->RAYMOND LULL. 167<-

...from the redness, but only to cook with the fire of the stone, until the whole becomes **red** and **fixed**, and nothing of it can be sublimated <note>original: *sublimari*; the process of turning a solid into vapor through heat, then back to solid.</note> into **white sulfur** <note>original: *sulphur album*; representing the penultimate stage of the work before the final reddening.</note>, which will be the sign of completed **decoction** <term>Decoction: <note>original: *decoctio*; a technical term for the long, slow "cooking" of the alchemical matter to mature it.</note></term>. 

Be careful, however, in the manner of doing and understanding the effect of the agent—namely, the **reddening fire**. For because the power of the stone’s fire existing in the seed of the **Sun** <note>original: *Solis*; Gold, the active masculine principle.</note> overcomes the power of the pure air of the white sulfur in the seed of the **Moon** <note>original: *Lunae*; Silver, the passive feminine principle.</note>, the pure white sulfur will become like the Sun in its redness and even in its weight. 

For just as a father generates his son in the woman’s seed, so that through the son he breathes forth the spirit with which we tinge with an **inseparable tincture** <term>Tincture: <note>original: *tinctura*; the concentrated essence or "dye" that transforms the properties of a base metal into those of a noble one.</note></term>, since love is the bond and the connection of both. This bond, which proceeds through the son from the part of the **red sulfur**, exists for the perfecting of everything imperfect—namely, into a true gold-making substance. 

And by this it is revealed to you that **circular perfection** <note>original: *perfectio circularis*; a reference to the Ouroboros or the cycle of distillation and return, signifying the wholeness of the Great Work.</note> shines back in the **mastery** <term>Mastery: <note>original: *magisterio*; the complete knowledge and execution of the alchemical process.</note></term> with the completion of the stone: by means of which, through the following operation, we make the complete **Elixir**.

# ON THE FOURTH AND
# FINAL PRACTICAL
### principle: in which is determined the power of the second reduction of water <note>original: *reductionis aquæ*; the process of returning a substance to a liquid or prime state for further refinement.</note>, which is handed down with the intention of fixing <note>original: *figendi*; making a substance "fixed" or stable so it no longer evaporates or "flies" when heated.</note>, and of washing the body, so that the whole might be ferment <term>Ferment: <note>original: *fermentum*; a substance that, like yeast in bread, has the power to transform and "leaven" the entire mass of metal into gold or silver.</note></term>.

->*Here begins the fourth Practical principle.*<-

### CHAPTER LXXIV.

The intention of this fourth and final practical principle is to fix **white sulfur** and **red sulfur** <note>original: *sulphur album & rubeum*; the two essential stages of the alchemical medicine: white for making silver, and red for making gold.</note> upon a **fixed body**: specifically, so that white sulfur is fixed upon **silver** <note>original: *argentum*.</note>, and red sulfur is fixed upon **gold** <note>original: *aurum*.</note>. For whoever does not coagulate <note>original: *coagulat*; to thicken or solidify a liquid.</note> the **quicksilver** <term>Quicksilver: <note>original: *argentum vivum*; Mercury, considered the "mother" of all metals and the primary ingredient in the work.</note></term>—once it has been extracted from bodies and converted into sulfur—into a **fixed white sulfur** that can endure the fire, certainly misses the path to **whiteness** <note>original: *albedinem*; the stage of the work where the matter becomes pure and capable of transforming metals into silver.</note>. And he who [leads] that same quicksilver into white sulfur...

<summary>This page introduces the final practical stage of the alchemical process, focusing on "fixing" white and red sulfur onto silver and gold to create a transformative ferment. It emphasizes that stabilizing quicksilver into a heat-resistant sulfur is essential for achieving the stage of "whiteness."</summary>
<keywords>fixation, white sulfur, red sulfur, ferment, quicksilver, silver, gold, coagulation, albedo</keywords>

...changed <note>Continuing from the previous page: if the mercury is not changed...</note>, it does not coagulate into a fixed **red sulfur** that can endure fire; it certainly finds no path to the redness <note>original: *rubedinem*; the final, highest stage of the alchemical work, producing gold.</note>. Son, you must be careful when you are in this conjunction: because without a **ferment**, neither **Sol** <note>original: *Sol*; the Sun, representing **Gold**.</note> nor **Luna** <note>original: *Luna*; the Moon, representing **Silver**.</note> will ever come forth. And that is because a 
<margin>The place is the womb</margin>
certain and determined place generates a certain and determined thing placed within it, through its own properties. For which reason we reveal to you, that the nature of all things having a **complexion** <term>Complexion: <note>original: *complexionalorum*; referring to the specific balance of the four qualities—hot, cold, wet, dry—that define a substance's physical and spiritual makeup.</note></term>, whether they be plants, or animals, or minerals, leans toward the nature of the complexion of the place of their generation.

Therefore, join the **sulfur** with the **body** from which it was prepared from the beginning, so that it may generate its own likeness. Let the whole **Elixir** be made of that which you join with what is of its own nature; for it rejects anything foreign when exposed to fire. When, therefore, you have joined the sulfur with its own body, that sulfur will never cease to act upon the other, until it converts the whole. For just as the moist part is hardened through the **first reduction**, by mixing in a vaporous way with the earthy vapor and is smothered <note>original: *praefocatur*; literally "choked," meaning the volatile vapors are suppressed or "fixed" into a solid state.</note> so that it becomes a sulfur similar to the earth, which is its **matrix** <note>original: *matrix*; a womb or source-environment.</note> or nurse, so also that sulfur, through its **second reduction**, is converted into the proper matter of the metal...

<summary>This page explains that the alchemical Elixir requires a "ferment" of gold or silver to succeed, as every substance is shaped by the environment (matrix) of its creation. It describes the "reduction" process where vapors are fixed into a solid, earthy state to transform the metallic body.</summary>

...is possessed <note>original: *potitur*; completing the sentence from the previous page.</note>, and is freely changed into the nature of the **ferment** <term>Ferment: <note>In alchemy, a "ferment" acts like a sourdough starter; it is a small amount of perfected substance (gold or silver) added to the mixture to "infect" the whole mass with its own characteristics.</note></term>, through the property of its addition. First, when you wish to ferment, mix the **sulfur** with the **body**, so that the whole may become a ferment. For the ferment brings our sulfur back to its own nature, color, and taste in every possible way. 

Do not think, however, that I called this conjunction the *first* fermentation, but rather a specific kind: insofar as it restores the **Stone** to the specific suitability of a certain temperament. Here, the body is simplified and prepared in the manner of a powder—calcined <note>original: *calcinati*; reduced to a powder through heat.</note>, dissolved, hardened, and fixed—so that it may take up again its own proper ferment, which we call **oil**. 

Let the ferment for the **white** work <note>The process for making Silver.</note> be white; and for the **red** work <note>The process for making Gold.</note>, let it be red. 

If, however, you place or prepare the body of **silver** with the sulfur of **gold**: it will be reduced into the nature of silver, but not to the color of gold; and vice versa, there will be a confusion of natures in the **mastery** <term>Mastery: <note>original: *magisterio*; the successful completion of the alchemical Great Work.</note></term>. Therefore, take care that you do not place the ferment or the body of one sulfur with the sulfur of another. Because the ferment of gold converts nothing into gold except its own **red sulfur**. Nor does the ferment of silver convert anything into silver except its own **white sulfur**. Because that can never...
